Explicit Relations between Kaneko–Yamamoto Type Multiple Zeta Values and Related Variants
Ce Xu and Jianqiang Zhao[1mm] a. School of Mathematics and Statistics, Anhui Normal University, Wuhu 241000, PRCb. Department of Mathematics, The Bishop’s School, La Jolla, CA 92037, USA[5mm] Dedicated to Professor Masanobu Kaneko on the occasion of his 60th birthday

Abstract




Abstract.
In this paper we first establish several integral identities. These integrals are of the form

\int_{0}^{1}x^{an+b}f(x)\,dx\quad(a\in\{1,2\},\ b\in\{-1,-2\})

where
f(x)
is a single-variable multiple polylogarithm function or
r
-variable multiple polylogarithm function or Kaneko–Tsumura A-function (this is a single-variable multiple polylogarithm function of level two). We find that these integrals can be expressed in terms of multiple zeta (star) values and their related variants (multiple
t
-values, multiple
T
-values, multiple
S
-values etc.), and multiple harmonic (star) sums and their related variants (multiple
T
-harmonic sums, multiple
S
-harmonic sums etc.), which are closely related to some special types of Schur multiple zeta values and their generalization. Using these integral identities, we prove many explicit evaluations of Kaneko–Yamamoto multiple zeta values and their related variants. Further, we derive some relations involving multiple zeta (star) values and their related variants.

Keywords
: Multiple zeta (star) values, multiple
t
-values, multiple
T
-values, multiple
M
-values, Kaneko–Yamamoto multiple zeta values, Schur multiple zeta values.

AMS Subject Classifications (2020):
11M06, 11M32, 11M99, 11G55.

1
Introduction and Notations

1.1
Multiple zeta values (MZVs) and Schur MZVs

We begin with some basic notations. A finite sequence
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}\equiv{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}:=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
of positive integers is called a
composition
. We put

|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}|:=k_{1}+\dotsb+k_{r},\quad{\rm dep}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}):=r,

and call them the weight and the depth of
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}
, respectively.

For
0\leq j\leq i
, we adopt the following notations:

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{i}^{j}:=(\underbrace{k_{i+1-j},k_{i+2-j},\dotsc,k_{i}}_{j\ \text{components}})

and

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{i}\equiv{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{i}^{i}:=(k_{1},k_{2},\dotsc,k_{i}),

where
{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{i}^{0}:=\emptyset

(i\geq 0)
. If
i<j
, then
{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{i}^{j}:=\emptyset
.

For a composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
and positive integer
n
, the multiple harmonic sums (MHSs) and multiple harmonic star sums (MHSSs) are defined by

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}):=\sum\limits_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}\leq n}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}m_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}

and

\displaystyle\zeta^{\star}_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}):=\sum\limits_{0<m_{1}\leq\cdots\leq m_{r}\leq n}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}m_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}},

respectively. If
n<r
then
{\zeta_{n}}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}):=0
and
{\zeta_{n}}(\emptyset)={\zeta^{\star}_{n}}(\emptyset):=1
.
The multiple zeta values (abbr. MZVs) and the multiple zeta-star value (abbr. MZSVs) are defined by

\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}):=\lim_{n\to\infty}\zeta_{n}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\zeta^{\star}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}):=\lim_{n\to\infty}\zeta_{n}^{\star}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}),

respectively. These series converge if and only if
k_{r}\geq 2
so we call a composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}

admissible
if this is the case.

The systematic study of multiple zeta values began in the early 1990s with the works of Hoffman
[
4
]
and Zagier
[
17
]
. Due to their surprising and sometimes mysterious appearance in the study of many branches of mathematics and theoretical physics, these special values have attracted a lot of attention and interest in the past three decades (for example, see the book by the second author
[
19
]
). A common generalization of the MZVs and MZSVs is given
by the Schur multiple zeta values
[
10
,
11
]
, which are defined using the skew Young tableaux.
For example, for integers
a,c,d,e,f\geq 1,\,c,g\geq 2
the following sum is an example of a Schur multiple mixed values

\zeta\left(\ {\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$a$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$b$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$c$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$d$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$e$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$f$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$g$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont\crcr}}\kern 910.88815pt}}$}}\ \right)=\sum_{{\scriptsize\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}&&m_{a}&\leq&m_{b}&\leq&m_{c}\\
&&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$<$}&&&&\\
m_{d}&\leq&m_{e}&&&&\\
\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$<$}&&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$<$}&&&\\
m_{f}&\leq&m_{g}&&&&\end{array}}}\frac{1}{m_{a}^{\,a}\,\,m_{b}^{b}\,\,m_{c}^{c}\,\,m_{d}^{d}\,\,m_{e}^{e}\,\,m_{f}^{f}}\,,

(1)

In this paper, we shall study some families of variations of MZVs. First, consider the following special form of
the Schur multiple zeta values.

Definition 1.1
.

(cf.
[
8
]
)
For any two compositions of positive integers
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
and
{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}=(l_{1},\dotsc,l_{s})
, define

\displaystyle\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}^{\star}}):=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}=n_{s}\geq\cdots\geq n_{1}>0}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}n_{1}^{l_{1}}\cdots n_{s}^{l_{s}}}

(2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1})\zeta^{\star}_{n}(l_{1},\dotsc,l_{s-1})}{n^{k_{r}+l_{s}}}.

We call them
Kaneko–Yamamoto multiple zeta values
(K–Y MZVs for short).

The K–Y MZVs defined by (
2
) are the special cases of the Schur multiple zeta values

\zeta_{\lambda}({\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}})
given by the following skew Young tableaux of anti-hook type

{\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}}={\footnotesize\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to15.64017pt{\vss\hbox to15.30023pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.64017pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 15.30023pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 15.30023pt\kern 0.33994pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to15.64017pt{\vss\hbox to15.30023pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.64017pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 15.30023pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 15.30023pt\kern 0.33994pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to15.64017pt{\vss\hbox to15.30023pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.64017pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 15.30023pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-15.64017pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 15.30023pt\kern 0.33994pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to15.64017pt{\vss\hbox to15.30023pt{\hss$\hbox to6.84pt{\vbox to4.73pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.41808pt\lower-2.36444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

where
x=k_{r}+l_{s}
and
{\lambda}
is simply the Young diagram underlying the above tableaux.

1.2
Variations of MZVs with even/odd summation indices

One may modify the definition MZVs by restricting the summation indices to even/odd numbers.
These values are apparently NOT in the class of Schur multiple zeta values.
For instance, in recent papers
[
6
,
7
]
, Kaneko and Tsumura introduced a new kind of
multiple zeta values of level two, called
multiple T-values
(MTVs), defined
for admissible compositions
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
by

\displaystyle T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}):

\displaystyle=2^{r}\sum_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}\atop m_{i}\equiv i\ {\rm mod}\ 2}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}m_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle=2^{r}\sum\limits_{0<n_{1}<\cdots<n_{r}}\frac{1}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-2)^{k_{2}}\cdots(2n_{r}-r)^{k_{r}}}.

(3)

This is in contrast to Hoffman’s
multiple
t
-values
(MtVs) defined in
[
5
]
as follows: for
admissible compositions
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})

\displaystyle t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}):=\sum_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}\atop\forall m_{i}:odd}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}m_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}=\sum\limits_{0<n_{1}<\cdots<n_{r}}\frac{1}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}}\cdots(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}}.

Moreover, in
[
5
]
Hoffman also defined its star version, called
multiple
t
-star value
(MtSVs), as follows:

\displaystyle t^{\star}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}):=\sum_{0<m_{1}\leq\cdots\leq m_{r}\atop\forall m_{i}:odd}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}m_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}=\sum\limits_{0<n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{r}}\frac{1}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}}\cdots(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}}.

Very recently, the authors have defined another variant of multiple zeta values in
[
13
]
, called
multiple mixed values
or
multiple
M
-values
(MMVs for short). For
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=(\varepsilon_{1},\dots,\varepsilon_{r})\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}

and admissible compositions
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
,

\displaystyle M({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}):

\displaystyle=\sum_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}}\frac{(1+\varepsilon_{1}(-1)^{m_{1}})\cdots(1+\varepsilon_{r}(-1)^{m_{r}})}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}\atop 2|m_{j}\ \text{if}\ \varepsilon_{j}=1\ \text{and}\ 2\nmid m_{j}\ \text{if}\ \varepsilon_{j}=-1}\frac{2^{r}}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}m_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}.

(4)

For brevity, we put a check on top of the component
k_{j}
if
\varepsilon_{j}=-1
. For example,

\displaystyle M(1,2,\check{3})=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{0<m_{1}<m_{2}<m_{3}}\frac{(1+(-1)^{m_{1}})(1+(-1)^{m_{2}})(1-(-1)^{m_{3}})}{m_{1}m_{2}^{2}m_{3}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{0<l<m<n}\frac{8}{(2\ell)(2m)^{2}(2n-1)^{3}}.

It is obvious that MtVs satisfy the series stuffle relation, however, it is nontrivial to see that MTVs can be expressed using iterated integral and satisfy both the duality relations (see
[
7
, Theorem 3.1]
) and the integral shuffle relations (see
[
7
, Theorem 2.1]
). Similar to MZVs, MMVs satisfy both series stuffle relations and the integral shuffle relations. Moreover, in
[
13
]
, we also introduced and studied
a class of MMVs that is opposite to MTVs, called
multiple S-values
(MSVs). For admissible compositions
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
,

\displaystyle S({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}):

\displaystyle=2^{r}\sum_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}\atop m_{i}\equiv i-1\ {\rm mod}\ 2}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}m_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle=2^{r}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\cdots<n_{r}}\frac{1}{(2n_{1})^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}}\cdots(2n_{r}-r+1)^{k_{r}}}.

(5)

It is clear that every MMV can be written as a linear combination of alternating MZVs
(also referred to as Euler sums or colored multiple zeta values) defined as follows.
For
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
and
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}
, if
(k_{r},{\varepsilon}_{r})\neq(1,1)
(called
admissible
case) then

\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}):=\sum\limits_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}}\frac{{\varepsilon}_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots{\varepsilon}_{r}^{m_{r}}}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}.

We may compactly indicate the presence of an alternating sign as follows.
Whenever
{\varepsilon}_{j}=-1
, we place a bar over the corresponding integer exponent
k_{j}
. For example,

\zeta(\bar{2},3,\bar{1},4)=\zeta(2,3,1,4;-1,1,-1,1).

Similarly, a star-version of alternating MZVs (called
alternating multiple zeta-star values
) is defined by

\zeta^{\star}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}):=\sum\limits_{0<m_{1}\leq\cdots\leq m_{r}}\frac{{\varepsilon}_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots{\varepsilon}_{r}^{m_{r}}}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}.

Deligne showed that the rational spaced generated by alternating MZVs of weight
w
is bounded by the Fibonacci number
F_{w}

where
F_{0}=F_{1}=1
. In
[
13
, Theorem 7.1]
we showed that the rational spaced generated by MMVs of weight
w

is bounded by
F_{w}-1
. The missing piece is the one-dimensional space generated by
\ln^{w}2
.

1.3
Variations of Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs with even/odd summation indices

Now, we introduce the
T
-variant of Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs.
For positive integers
m
and
n
such that
n\geq m
, we define

\displaystyle D_{n,m}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Big\{(n_{1},n_{2},\dotsc,n_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\mid 0<n_{1}\leq n_{2}<n_{3}\leq\cdots\leq n_{m-1}<n_{m}\leq n\Big\},&\hbox{if $2\nmid m$;}\\
\Big\{(n_{1},n_{2},\dotsc,n_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\mid 0<n_{1}\leq n_{2}<n_{3}\leq\cdots<n_{m-1}\leq n_{m}<n\Big\},&\hbox{if $2\mid m$,}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle E_{n,m}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Big\{(n_{1},n_{2},\dotsc,n_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\mid 1\leq n_{1}<n_{2}\leq n_{3}<\cdots<n_{m-1}\leq n_{m}<n\Big\},&\hbox{if $2\nmid m$;}\\
\Big\{(n_{1},n_{2},\dotsc,n_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\mid 1\leq n_{1}<n_{2}\leq n_{3}<\cdots\leq n_{m-1}<n_{m}\leq n\Big\},&\hbox{if $2\mid m$.}\end{array}\right.

Definition 1.2
.

(
[
13
, Defn. 1.1]
) For positive integer
m
, define

\displaystyle T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}}):=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{n}}}\in D_{n,2m-1}}\frac{2^{2m-1}}{(\prod_{j=1}^{m-1}(2n_{2j-1}-1)^{k_{2j-1}}(2n_{2j})^{k_{2j}})(2n_{2m-1}-1)^{k_{2m-1}}},

(6)

\displaystyle T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}}):=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{n}}}\in D_{n,2m}}\frac{2^{2m}}{\prod_{j=1}^{m}(2n_{2j-1}-1)^{k_{2j-1}}(2n_{2j})^{k_{2j}}},

(7)

\displaystyle S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}}):=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{n}}}\in E_{n,2m-1}}\frac{2^{2m-1}}{(\prod_{j=1}^{m-1}(2n_{2j-1})^{k_{2j-1}}(2n_{2j}-1)^{k_{2j}})(2n_{2m-1})^{k_{2m-1}}},

(8)

\displaystyle S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}}):=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{n}}}\in E_{n,2m}}\frac{2^{2m}}{\prod_{j=1}^{m}(2n_{2j-1})^{k_{2j-1}}(2n_{2j}-1)^{k_{2j}}},

(9)

where
T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}}):=0
if
n<m
, and
T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}})=S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}})=S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}}):=0
if
n\leq m
. Moreover, for convenience sake, we set
T_{n}(\emptyset)=S_{n}(\emptyset):=1
. We call (
6
) and (
7
)
multiple
T
-harmonic sums
(MTHSs for short), and call (
8
) and (
9
)
multiple
S
-harmonic sums
(MSHSs for short).

In
[
13
]
, we used the MTHSs and MSHSs to define the convoluted
T
-values
T({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}})
, which can be regarded as a
S
- or
T
-variant of K–Y MZVs.

Definition 1.3
.

(
[
13
, Defn. 1.2]
) For positive integers
m
and
p
, the
convoluted
T
-values
are defined by

\displaystyle T({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p}})=

\displaystyle\,2\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}})T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-1}})}{(2n)^{k_{2m}+l_{2p}}},

(10)

\displaystyle T({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-1}})=

\displaystyle\,2\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2}})T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-2}})}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m-1}+l_{2p-1}}},

(11)

\displaystyle T({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-1}})=

\displaystyle\,2\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}})S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-2}})}{(2n)^{k_{2m}+l_{2p-1}}},

(12)

\displaystyle T({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p}})=

\displaystyle\,2\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2}})S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-1}})}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m-1}+l_{2p}}}.

(13)

We may further define the
convoluted
S
-values
by

\displaystyle S({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p}})=

\displaystyle\,2\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}})S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-1}})}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m}+l_{2p}}},

(14)

\displaystyle S({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-1}})=

\displaystyle\,2\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2}})S_{n}({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{2p-2}})}{(2n)^{k_{2m-1}+l_{2p-1}}}.

(15)

In view of the interpretation of K–Y MZVs as special Schur MZVs, one may wonder if
Schur MZVs can be generalized so that the convoluted
S
- and
T
-values are special cases.

1.4
Schur MZVs modulo
N

We now generalize the concept of Schur multiple zeta functions (resp. values)
to Schur multiple zeta functions (resp. values) modulo any positive integer
N
,
the case
N=2
of which contain all the MMVs as special cases.

It turns out that when
N=2
the only differences between these values and the Schur MZVs is
that each box in the Young diagram is decorated by either “0” or “1” at upper left corner so that the running
index appearing in that box must be either even or odd. For example, a variation of
the example in (
1
) can be given as follows:

\zeta\left(\ {\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss${}^{\text{0}}a$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss${}^{\text{1}}c$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss${}^{\text{1}}d$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss${}^{\text{1}}e$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss${}^{\text{0}}f$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss${}^{\text{1}}g$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont\crcr}}\kern 910.88815pt}}$}}\ \right):=\sum_{{\scriptsize\begin{array}[]{cccccccl}&&m_{a}&\leq&m_{b}&\leq&m_{c}&\ 2|m_{a},2|m_{b},2\nmid m_{c}\\
&&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$<$}&&&&&\\
m_{d}&\leq&m_{e}&&&&&\ 2\nmid m_{d},2\nmid m_{e}\\
\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$<$}&&\rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$<$}&&&&\\
m_{f}&\leq&m_{g}&&&&&\ 2|m_{f},2\nmid m_{g}\end{array}}}\frac{2^{7}}{m_{a}^{\,a}\,\,m_{b}^{b}\,\,m_{c}^{c}\,\,m_{d}^{d}\,\,m_{e}^{e}\,\,m_{f}^{f}}\,,

We now briefly describe this idea in general. For a skew Young diagram

{\lambda}
with
n
boxes (denoted by
\sharp({\lambda})=n
),
let
T({\lambda},X)
be the set of all Young tableaux of shape
{\lambda}
over a set
X
.
Let
D({\lambda})=\{(i,j):1\leq i\leq r,{\alpha}_{i}\leq j\leq{\beta}_{i}\}=\{(i,j):1\leq j\leq s,a_{j}\leq i\leq b_{j}\}
be the skew Young diagram of
{\lambda}

so that
(i,j)
refers to the box on the
i
th row and
j
th column of
{\lambda}
.
Fix any positive integer
N
, we may decorate
D({\lambda})
by putting a residue class
\pi_{ij}
mod
N
at
the upper left corner of
(i,j)
-th box. We call such a decorated diagram a Young diagram modulo
N
,
denoted by
{\lambda}^{\pi}
. Further, we define the set of semi-standard skew Young tableaux of shape
{\lambda}^{\pi}
by

{\text{SSYT}}({\lambda}^{\pi}):=\left\{(m_{i,j})\in T({\lambda},\mathbb{N})\left|\begin{aligned} &m_{i,{\alpha}_{i}}\leq m_{i,{\alpha}_{i}+1}\leq\dotsm\leq m_{i,{\beta}_{i}},\ \ m_{a_{j},j}<m_{a_{j}+1,j}<\dotsm<m_{b_{j},j},\\
&m_{i,j}\equiv\pi_{i,j}\pmod{N}\ \ \forall 1\leq i\leq r,{\alpha}_{i}\leq j\leq{\beta}_{i}\end{aligned}\right.\right\}.

For
{\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}}=(s_{i,j})\in T({\lambda},\mathbb{C})
, the
Schur multiple zeta function

modulo

N

associated with
{\lambda}^{\pi}
is defined by the series

\zeta_{{\lambda}^{\pi}}({\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}}):=\sum_{M\in{\text{SSYT}}({\lambda}^{\pi})}\frac{2^{\sharp({\lambda})}}{M^{\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}}}

where
M^{\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}}=(m_{i,j})^{\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}}:=\prod{}_{(i,j)\in D({\lambda})}m_{i,j}^{s_{i,j}}
.
Similar to
[
11
, Lemma 2.1]
, it is not too hard to prove that the above series converges
absolutely whenever
{\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}}\in W_{\lambda}
where

W_{\lambda}:=\left\{{\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}}=(s_{i,j})\in T({\lambda},\mathbb{C})\left|\begin{aligned} &\Re(s_{i,j})\geq 1\ \forall(i,j)\in D({\lambda})\setminus C({\lambda})\\
&\Re(s_{i,j})>1\ \forall(i,j)\in C({\lambda})\end{aligned}\right.\right\},

where
C({\lambda})
is the set of all corners of
{\lambda}
. But this domain of convergence is not ideal. To define
the most accurate domain of convergence we need the following terminology. Given any two boxes
B_{1}
and
B_{2}

in
{\lambda}
we define
B_{1}\preceq B_{2}
if
B_{1}
is to the left or above
B_{2}
, namely,
B_{1}

must be in the gray area in the picture

where the
B_{2}
is the black box.

An
allowable move
along a path from box
B
is a move to a box
C
such that
B\preceq C
and all boxes above
C
and to the left of
C
, if there are any, are already covered by the previous moves along the path.
An
allowable path
in a skew Young diagram is a sequence of allowable moves covering all
the boxes without backtracking.
Then the domain of convergence of
M_{{\lambda}^{\pi}}({\boldsymbol{\sl{s}}})
is the subset of
W_{\lambda}

defined by the condition that
\Re(\sum_{s_{ij}\in\mathcal{P}_{\ell}}s_{ij})>\ell
for all allowable paths

\mathcal{P}
, where
\mathcal{P}_{\ell}
is the sub-path of
\mathcal{P}
covering the last
\ell
boxes ending at a corner.
For example, the graph

4

\scriptscriptstyle 6

\scriptscriptstyle 2

\scriptscriptstyle 3

(
1\to 2\to\cdots\to 7
) shows an allowable path in a skew Young diagram.

Similar to K–Y MZVs, the above convoluted
S
- and
T
-values are all special cases of Schur MZVs modulo 2 corresponding to anti-hook type Young diagrams. The six convoluted
S
- or
T
-values in
(
14
)-(
15
) are all given by mod 2 Schur MZVs

\zeta_{{\lambda}_{j}^{\pi_{j}}}
(
1\leq j\leq 6
) below, respectively:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lambda}_{1}^{\pi_{1}}={\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to18.39998pt{\vss\hbox to18.00005pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-18.39998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 18.00005pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 18.00005pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to18.39998pt{\vss\hbox to18.00005pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-18.39998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 18.00005pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 18.00005pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to18.39998pt{\vss\hbox to18.00005pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-18.39998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 18.00005pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 18.00005pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to18.39998pt{\vss\hbox to18.00005pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-18.39998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 18.00005pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-18.39998pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 18.00005pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to18.39998pt{\vss\hbox to18.00005pt{\hss$\hbox to3.33pt{\vbox to1pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.66496pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} where
m^{\prime}=2m-1,m^{\prime\prime}=2m-2,p^{\prime}=2p-1,p^{\prime\prime}=2p-2
,
x_{1}=k_{2m}+l_{2p}
,
x_{2}=k_{2m-1}+l_{2p-1}
,
x_{3}=k_{2m}+l_{2p-1}
, and
x_{4}=k_{2m-1}+l_{2p}
.

The primary goals of this paper are to study the explicit relations of K–Y MZVs
\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}^{\star}})
and their related variants, such as
T
-variants
T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}})
. Then using these explicit relations, we establish some explicit formulas of multiple zeta (star) values and their related variants.

The remainder of this paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we first establish the explicit evaluations of integrals
\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x)\,dx
and
\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n+b}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};x)\,dx
for all positive integers
n
and
b\in\{-1,-2\}
, where
{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x)
is the single-variable multiple polylogarithm (see (
17
))
and
{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};x)
is the Kaneko–Tsumura A-function (see (
24
)). Then, for all compositions
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}
, using these explicit formulas obtained and by considering the two kind of integrals

I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}):=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x){\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}(x)}{x}\,dx\quad\text{and}\quad I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}):=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};x){\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};x)}{x}\,dx,

we establish some explicit relations of
\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}^{\star}})
and
T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}\circledast{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}})
. Further, we express the integrals
I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
and
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
in terms of multiple integrals associated with 2-labeled posets following the idea of Yamamoto
[
14
]
.

In Section
3
, we first define a variation of the classical multiple polylogarithm function with
r
-variable

{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x_{1},x_{2},\dotsc,x_{r})
(see (
39
)), and give the explicit evaluation of the integral

\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(\sigma_{1}x,\sigma_{2}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r}x)\,dx,\quad\sigma_{j}\in\{\pm 1\}.

Then we will consider the integral

I_{\lambda}(({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}),({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})):=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(\sigma_{1}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r}x){\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{s}}(\varepsilon_{1}x,\dotsc,\varepsilon_{s}x)}{x}\,dx

to find some explicit relations of alternating Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs
\zeta(({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}})\circledast({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})^{\star})
. Further, we will find some relations involving alternating MZVs. Finally, we express the integrals
I_{\lambda}(({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}),({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}))
in terms of multiple integrals associated with 3-labeled posets.

In Section
4
, we define the multiple
t
-harmonic (star) sums and the function
t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};x)
related to multiple
t
-values. Further, we establish some relations involving multiple
t
-star values.

2
Formulas of Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs and
T
-Variants

In this section we will prove several explicit formulas of Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs and
T
-variants, and find some explicit relations among MZ(S)Vs and MTVs.

2.1
Some relations of Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs

Theorem 2.1
.

Let
r,n\in\mathbb{N}
and
{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}:=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\rm Li}_{{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{n^{j}}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j\right)+\frac{(-1)^{|{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}|-r}}{n^{k_{r}}}\zeta^{\star}_{n}\left(1,{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}^{l}|-l}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r-l}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{n^{k_{r}}}\zeta^{\star}_{n}\left(j,{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-1}^{l-1}\right)\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-l-1},k_{r-l}+1-j\right),

(16)

where
{{\rm Li}}_{{{k_{1}},\dotsc,{k_{r}}}}(z)
is the single-variable multiple polylogarithm function defined by

\displaystyle{{\rm Li}}_{{{k_{1}},\dotsc,{k_{r}}}}(z):=\sum\limits_{0<{n_{1}}<\cdots<{n_{r}}}{\frac{{{z^{{n_{r}}}}}}{{n_{1}^{{k_{1}}}\cdots n_{r}^{{k_{r}}}}}},\quad z\in\left[{-1,1}\right).

(17)

Proof.

It’s well known that multiple polylogarithms can be expressed by the iterated integral

\displaystyle{\rm Li}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(x)=\int_{0}^{x}\frac{dt}{1-t}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{1}-1}\dotsm\frac{dt}{1-t}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{r}-1},

where for 1-forms
{\alpha}_{1}(t)=f_{1}(t)\,dt,\dotsc,{\alpha}_{\ell}(t)=f_{\ell}(t)\,dt
, we define iteratively

\int_{a}^{b}{\alpha}_{1}(t)\cdots{\alpha}_{\ell}(t)=\int_{a}^{b}\left(\int_{a}^{y}{\alpha}_{1}(t)\cdots{\alpha}_{\ell-1}(t)\right)f_{\ell}(y)\,dy.

Using integration by parts, we deduce the recurrence relation

\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\rm Li}_{{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}}(x)dx=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{n^{j}}\zeta({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}-1}}{n^{k_{r}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}x^{j-1}{\rm Li}_{{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-1}}(x)dx.

Thus, we arrive at the desired formula by a direct calculation.
∎

For any string
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{d}\}
and
r\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{d}\}_{r}

the concatenated string obtained by repeating
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{d}\}
exactly
r
times.

Corollary 2.2
.

(cf.
[
12
]
)
For positive integers
n
and
r
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}\log^{r}(1-x)dx=(-1)^{r}r!\frac{\zeta^{\star}_{n}(\{1\}_{r})}{n}.

For any nontrivial compositions
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}
,
we consider the integral

I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}):=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x){\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}(x)}{x}\,dx

and use (
2.1
) to find some explicit relations of K–Y MZVs. We prove the following theorem.

Theorem 2.3
.

For compositions
{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
and
{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s}=(l_{1},l_{2},\dotsc,l_{s})\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j\right)\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s-1},l_{s}+j\right)+(-1)^{|{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}|-r}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s}\circledast\Big(1,{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}\Big)^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}^{i}|-i}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r-i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-i-1},k_{r-i}+1-j\right)\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s}\circledast\Big(j,{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}^{i}\Big)^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{l_{s}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s-1},l_{s}+1-j\right)\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+j\right)+(-1)^{|{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s}|-s}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}\circledast\Big(1,{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s}\Big)^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}(-1)^{|{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s}^{i}|-i}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{s-i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s-i-1},l_{s-i}+1-j\right)\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}\circledast\Big(j,{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s}^{i}\Big)^{\star}\right).

(18)

Proof.

According to the definition of multiple polylogarithm, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(x){\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{s}}(x)}{x}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{s-1})}{n^{l_{s}}}\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1})}{n^{k_{r}}}\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\rm Li}_{{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}_{s}}(x)dx

Then using (
2.1
) with a direct calculation, we may deduce the desired evaluation.
∎

The formula in Theorem
2.3
seems to be related to the harmonic product of Schur MZVs of anti-hook type
in
[
10
, Theorem 3.2]
and the general harmonic product
formula in
[
3
, Lemma 2.2]
. However, it does not seem to follow
from them easily.

As a special case, setting
r=2,s=1
in (
2.3
) and noting the fact that

\zeta(l_{1}\circledast(1,k_{1},k_{2})^{\star})=\zeta^{\star}(1,k_{1},k_{2}+l_{1})

and

\zeta(l_{1}\circledast(j,k_{2})^{\star})=\zeta^{\star}(j,l_{1}+k_{2})

we find that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta(k_{1},k_{2}+1-j)\zeta(l_{1}+j)+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\zeta^{\star}(1,k_{1},k_{2}+l_{1})

\displaystyle+(-1)^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta(k_{1}+1-j)\zeta^{\star}(j,l_{1}+k_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{l_{1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta(l_{1}+1-j)\zeta(k_{1},k_{2}+j)+(-1)^{l_{1}-1}\zeta((k_{1},k_{2})\circledast(1,l_{1})^{\star}).

(19)

On the other hand, from the definition of K–Y MZVs, it is easy to find that

\zeta((k_{1},k_{2})\circledast(1,l_{1})^{\star})=\zeta^{\star}(k_{1},1,k_{2}+l_{1})+\zeta^{\star}(1,k_{1},k_{2}+l_{1})-\zeta^{\star}(k_{1}+1,k_{2}+l_{1})-\zeta^{\star}(1,k_{1}+k_{2}+l_{1}).

Hence, we can get the following corollary.

Corollary 2.4
.

For positive integers
k_{1},k_{2}
and
l_{1}
,

\displaystyle((-1)^{l_{1}-1}+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}-1})\zeta^{\star}(1,k_{1},k_{2}+l_{1})+(-1)^{l_{1}-1}\zeta^{\star}(k_{1},1,k_{2}+l_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta(k_{1},k_{2}+1-j)\zeta(l_{1}+j)-(-1)^{k_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta(k_{1}+1-j)\zeta^{\star}(j,l_{1}+k_{2})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta(l_{1}+1-j)\zeta(k_{1},k_{2}+j)+(-1)^{l_{1}-1}\zeta^{\star}(k_{1}+1,k_{2}+l_{1})

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{l_{1}-1}\zeta^{\star}(1,k_{1}+k_{2}+l_{1}).

(20)

Next, we establish an identity involving

Arakawa–Kaneko zeta function
(see
[
1
]
) which is defined by

\displaystyle\xi(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};s):=\frac{1}{\Gamma(s)}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{s-1}}{e^{t}-1}{{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(1-e^{-t})dt\quad(\Re(s)>0).

(21)

Setting variables
1-e^{-t}=x
and
s=p+1\in\mathbb{N}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\xi(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};p+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{p}}{p!}\int\limits_{0}^{1}\frac{\log^{p}(1-x){\mathrm{Li}}_{{{k_{1}},{k_{2}},\cdots,{k_{r}}}}\left(x\right)}{x}dx

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1})\zeta^{\star}_{n}(\{1\}_{p})}{n^{k_{r}+1}}=\zeta({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}\circledast(\{1\}_{p+1})^{\star}),

where we have used Corollary
2.2
. Clearly, the Arakawa–Kaneko zeta value is a special case of integral
I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
.
Further, setting
l_{1}=l_{2}=\cdots=l_{s}=1
in Theorem
2.3
yields

\displaystyle\xi(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};s+1)=\zeta({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}\circledast(\{1\}_{s+1})^{\star})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j\right)\zeta\left(\{1\}_{s-1},1+j\right)+(-1)^{|{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}|-r}\zeta\left(\{1\}_{s}\circledast\Big(1,{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}\Big)^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}^{i}|-i}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r-i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta\left({{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r-i-1},k_{r-i}+1-j\right)\zeta\left(\{1\}_{s}\circledast\Big(j,{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}_{r}^{i}\Big)^{\star}\right).

We end this section by the following theorem and corollary.

Theorem 2.5
.

For any positive integer
m
and composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
,

\displaystyle 2\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}{\bar{\zeta}}(2m-1-2j)\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1})T_{n}(\{1\}_{2j+1})}{n^{k_{r}+1}}+\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1})S_{n}(\{1\}_{2m})}{n^{k_{r}+1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}(-1)^{j-1}2^{j}\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j)T(\{1\}_{2m-1},j+1)+(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}|-r}\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-1})\zeta^{\star}_{n}(1,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1})}{n^{k_{r}+1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}^{l}|-l}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r-l}-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-l-1},k_{r-l}+1-j)\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-1})\zeta^{\star}_{n}\Big(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l-1}\Big)}{n^{k_{r}+1}}.

(22)

Proof.

On the one hand, in
[
13
, Theorem 3.6]
, we proved that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{x}\cdot{{\rm Li}}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x^{2})\log^{2m}\left(\frac{1-x}{1+x}\right)\,dx=\frac{(2m)!}{2}\times[\text{The left-hand side of \eqref{czt}}].

On the other hand, we note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{x}\cdot{{\rm Li}}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x^{2})\log^{2m}\left(\frac{1-x}{1+x}\right)\,dx

\displaystyle=(2m)!\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-1})}{n}\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-1}{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x^{2})dx

\displaystyle=(2m)!\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-1})}{2n}\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x)dx.

Then using (
2.1
) with an elementary calculation, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{x}\cdot{{\rm Li}}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}(x^{2})\log^{2m}\left(\frac{1-x}{1+x}\right)\,dx=\frac{(2m)!}{2}\times[\text{The right-hand side of \eqref{czt}}].

Thus, formula (
2.5
) holds.
∎

In particular, setting
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(\{1\}_{r-1},k)
we obtain
[
13
, Theorem 3.9]
. Setting
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(\{2\}_{r-1},k)
we get the following corollary.

Corollary 2.6
.

For any positive integers
k,m
and
r
,

2\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}{\bar{\zeta}}(2m-1-2j)\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}(\{2\}_{r-1})T_{n}(\{1\}_{2j+1})}{n^{k+1}}+\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}(\{2\}_{r-1})S_{n}(\{1\}_{2m})}{n^{k+1}}\\
=\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{j-1}2^{j}\zeta(\{2\}_{r-1},k+1-j)T(\{1\}_{2m-1},j+1)\\
+\sum_{l=1}^{r}(-1)^{l+k}\zeta(\{2\}_{r-l})\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-1})\zeta^{\star}_{n}(j,\{2\}_{l-1})}{n^{k+1}}.

(23)

2.2
Some relations of
T
-varinat of Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs

Recall that the Kaneko–Tsumura A-function
{{\rm A}}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};z)
(see
[
6
]
) is defined by

\displaystyle{{\rm A}}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};z):=2^{r}\sum\limits_{1\leq{n_{1}}<\cdots<{n_{r}}\atop n_{i}\equiv i\ {\rm mod}\ 2}{\frac{{{z^{{n_{r}}}}}}{{n_{1}^{{k_{1}}}\cdots n_{r}^{{k_{r}}}}}},\quad z\in\left[{-1,1}\right).

(24)

In this subsection, we present a series of results concerning this function.

Theorem 2.7
.

For positive integers
m
and
n
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m};x)\,dx=\sum_{j=1}^{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1},k_{2m}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}|}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m}}}T_{n}(1,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m}}}\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2m-2i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i-1},k_{2m-2i}+1-j)T_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}^{2i-1})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m}}}\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i+1}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2m-2i-1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i-2},k_{2m-2i-1}+1-j)S_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}^{2i})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m}}}\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i+1}|}\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i-1},1;x)dx\right)T_{n}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}^{2i}),

(25)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1};x)\,dx=\sum_{j=1}^{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n)^{j}}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m},k_{2m+1}+1-j)-\frac{(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}|}}{(2n)^{k_{2m+1}}}T_{n}(1,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2n)^{k_{2m+1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}^{2i+1}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2m-2i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i-1},k_{2m-2i}+1-j)T_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n)^{k_{2m+1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}^{2i+2}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2m-2i-1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i-2},k_{2m-2i-1}+1-j)S_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i+1})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n)^{k_{2m+1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}^{2i+2}|}\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i-1},1;x)dx\right)T_{n}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i+1}),

(26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1};x)\,dx=\sum_{j=1}^{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m},k_{2m+1}+1-j)-\frac{(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}|}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m+1}}}S_{n}(1,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m+1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}^{2i}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2m+1-2i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i},k_{2m+1-2i}+1-j)T_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i-1})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m+1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}^{2i+1}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2m-2i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i-1},k_{2m-2i}+1-j)S_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{2m+1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{m}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m+1}^{2i+1}|}\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i},1;x)dx\right)T_{n}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i}),

(27)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m};x)\,dx=\sum_{j=1}^{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n)^{j}}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1},k_{2m}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}|}}{(2n)^{k_{2m}}}S_{n}(1,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2n)^{k_{2m}}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i-1}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2m+1-2i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i},k_{2m+1-2i}+1-j)T_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}^{2i-2})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n)^{k_{2m}}}\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2m-2i}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i-1},k_{2m-2i}+1-j)S_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}^{2i-1})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n)^{k_{2m}}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m}^{2i}|}\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-2i},1;x)dx\right)T_{n}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{2m-1}^{2i-1}),

(28)

where we allow
m=0
in (
26
) and (
27
).

Proof.

It is easy to see that the A-function can be expressed by an iterated integral:

\displaystyle{\rm A}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x)=\int_{0}^{x}\frac{2dt}{1-t^{2}}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{1}-1}\cdots\frac{2dt}{1-t^{2}}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{r}-1}.

Using integration by parts, we deduce the recurrence relation

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r};x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}-1}}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},1;x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}-1}}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}2\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\int_{0}^{1}x^{2k-1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1};x)\,dx,

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-1}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r};x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k_{r}-2}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{(2n)^{j+1}}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},k_{r}-j)+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}-1}}{(2n)^{k_{r}}}2\sum_{k=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}x^{2k-2}{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1};x)\,dx.

Hence, using the recurrence formulas above, we may deduce the four desired evaluations
after an elementary but rather tedious computation, which we leave to the interested reader.
∎

Lemma 2.8
.

For any positive integer
r
we have

\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(\{1\}_{r};x)\,dx=-2^{1-r}\zeta(\bar{r})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\log 2,&\hbox{if $r=1$;}\\
2^{1-r}(1-2^{1-r})\zeta(r),&\hbox{if $r\geq 2$.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Consider the generating function

G(u):=1+\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(\{1\}_{r};x)\,dx\right)(-2u)^{r}.

By definition

\displaystyle G(u)=\,

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}(-2u)^{r}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{x}\left(\frac{dt}{1-t^{2}}\right)^{r}\,dx

\displaystyle\ =\,

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-2u)^{r}}{r!}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{x}\frac{dt}{1-t^{2}}\right)^{r}\,dx

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 1+\int_{0}^{1}\left(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r!}\left(-u\log\left(\frac{1+x}{1-x}\right)\right)^{r}\right)\,dx

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{1-x}{1+x}\right)^{u}\,dx.

Taking
a=u,b=1,c=u+2
and
t=-1
in the formula

{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\left.{a,b\atop c}\right|t\right)=\frac{\Gamma(c)}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c-b)}\int_{0}^{1}v^{b-1}(1-v)^{c-b-1}(1-vt)^{-a}\,dv,

we obtain

\displaystyle G(u)=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{u+1}\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{u(u+1)}{(u+k)(u+k+1)}(-1)^{k}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle u\sum_{k\geq 0}(-1)^{k}\left(\frac{1}{u+k}-\frac{1}{u+k+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{k\geq 1}2(-1)^{k}\frac{u}{u+k}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 1-\sum_{k\geq 1}2(-1)^{k}\sum_{r\geq 0}\left(\frac{-u}{k}\right)^{r+1}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 1-2\sum_{r\geq 1}\zeta(\bar{r})(-u)^{r}.

The lemma follows immediately.
∎

Theorem 2.9
.

For composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
, the integral

\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;x)dx

can be expressed as a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of alternating MZVs.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the integral can be expressed in terms of
\log 2
and MMVs since these values
generate the same
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space as that by alternating MZVs as shown in
[
13
]
.
Suppose
k_{r}>1
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 2^{r+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<n_{1}<\cdots<n_{r}<n_{r+1}\\
n_{i}\equiv i\pmod{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{r+1}}-\frac{1}{n_{r+1}+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{*}(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,\breve{k_{r}},1)-M_{*}(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\dotsc,\breve{k_{r}},\breve{1}),&\qquad\hbox{if $2\nmid r$;}\\
M_{*}(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,k_{r},\breve{1})-M_{*}(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\dotsc,k_{r},1),&\qquad\hbox{if $2\mid r$,}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,\breve{k_{r}})\big(M_{*}(1)-M_{*}(\breve{1})\big)&\pmod{MMV},\qquad\hbox{if $2\nmid r$;}\\
M(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,k_{r})\big(M_{*}(\breve{1})-M_{*}(1)\big)&\pmod{MMV},\qquad\hbox{if $2\mid r$,}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-2M(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,\breve{k_{r}})\log 2&\pmod{MMV},\qquad\hbox{if $2\nmid r$;}\\
2M(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,k_{r})\log 2&\pmod{MMV},\qquad\hbox{if $2\mid r$.}\end{array}\right.

which can be expressed as a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of MMVs by
[
13
, Theorem 7.1]
.

In general, we may assume
k_{r}>1
and consider
\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},\{1\}_{\ell};x)\,dx
.
By induction on
\ell
, we see that

\displaystyle\,\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},\{1\}_{\ell};x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,\breve{k_{r}})\big(M_{*}({\boldsymbol{\sl{u}}},1)-M_{*}({\boldsymbol{\sl{u}}},\breve{1})\big)&\pmod{MMV},\qquad\hbox{if $2\nmid r$, $2\nmid\ell$;}\\
M(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,\breve{k_{r}})\big(M_{*}({\boldsymbol{\sl{u}}}^{\prime},1,\breve{1})-M_{*}({\boldsymbol{\sl{u}}}^{\prime},1,1)\big)&\pmod{MMV},\qquad\hbox{if $2\nmid r$, $2\mid\ell$;}\\
M(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,k_{r})\big(M_{*}({\boldsymbol{\sl{v}}},\breve{1})-M_{*}({\boldsymbol{\sl{v}}},1)\big)&\pmod{MMV},\qquad\hbox{if $2\mid r$, $2\nmid\ell$;}\\
M(\breve{k_{1}},k_{2},\breve{k_{3}},\dotsc,k_{r})\big(M_{*}({\boldsymbol{\sl{v}}}^{\prime},\breve{1},1)-M_{*}({\boldsymbol{\sl{v}}}^{\prime},\breve{1},\breve{1})\big)&\pmod{MMV},\qquad\hbox{if $2\mid r$, $2\mid\ell$,}\end{array}\right.

where
{\boldsymbol{\sl{u}}}=\{1,\breve{1}\}_{(\ell-1)/2},{\boldsymbol{\sl{u}}}^{\prime}=\{1,\breve{1}\}_{(\ell-2)/2},{\boldsymbol{\sl{v}}}=\{\breve{1},1\}_{(\ell-1)/2},{\boldsymbol{\sl{v}}}^{\prime}=\{\breve{1},1\}_{(\ell-2)/2}.
By Lemma
2.8
we see that

M_{*}(\cdots,1)-M_{*}(\cdots,\breve{1})=\mp 2\zeta(\bar{\ell})
. This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Example 2.10
.

Applying the idea in the proof of Theorem
2.9
we can find that for any positive integer
k
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k,1;x)\,dx

\displaystyle=M_{*}(\breve{k},1)-M_{*}(\breve{k},\breve{1})

\displaystyle=M(\breve{k})(M_{*}(1)-M_{*}(\breve{1}))+M(\breve{1},\breve{k})-M(1,\breve{k})+2M\big((k+1)\breve{\,}\big).

Observing that
M_{*}(\breve{1})-M_{*}(1)=2\log(2),\ M(\breve{k})=T(k),\ M(\breve{1},\breve{k})=4t(1,k)
and
M(1,\breve{k})=S(1,k)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k,1;x)\,dx=-2\log(2)T(k)+2T(k+1)+4t(1,k)-S(1,k).

From Lemma
2.8
we can get the following corollary, which was proved in
[
13
]
.

Corollary 2.11
.

(
[
13
, Theorem 3.1]
)
For positive integers
m
and
n
, the following identities hold.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{1}t^{2n-2}\log^{2m}\left(\frac{1-t}{1+t}\right)dt&=\frac{2(2m)!}{2n-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\bar{\zeta}}(2j)T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-2j}),\end{aligned}

(29)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{1}t^{2n-2}\log^{2m-1}\left(\frac{1-t}{1+t}\right)dt&=-\frac{(2m-1)!}{2n-1}\left(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\bar{\zeta}}(2j-1)T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-2j})+S_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-1})\right),\end{aligned}

(30)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{1}t^{2n-1}\log^{2m}\left(\frac{1-t}{1+t}\right)dt&=\frac{(2m)!}{n}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\bar{\zeta}}(2j-1)T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-2j+1})+S_{n}(\{1\}_{2m})\right),\end{aligned}

(31)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{1}t^{2n-1}\log^{2m-1}\left(\frac{1-t}{1+t}\right)dt&=-\frac{(2m-1)!}{n}\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}{\bar{\zeta}}(2j-2)T_{n}(\{1\}_{2m-2j-1}),\end{aligned}

(32)

where
{\bar{\zeta}}(m):=-\zeta(\overline{m})
, and
{\bar{\zeta}}(0)
should be interpreted as
1/2
wherever it occurs.

We now derive some explicit relations about
T
-variant of K–Y MZV
T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}\circledast{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
by considering the integral

I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}):=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};x){\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};x)}{x}\,dx.

Theorem 2.12
.

For positive integers
k
and
l
, we have

((-1)^{l}-(-1)^{k})S(1,k+l)=\sum_{j=1}^{l}(-1)^{j-1}T(l+1-j)T(k+j)+\sum_{j=1}^{k}(-1)^{j}T(k+1-j)T(l+j),

where
T(1):=2\log(2)
.

Proof.

One may deduce the formula by a straightforward calculation of the integral

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm A}(k;x){\rm A}(l;x)}{x}\,dx.

We leave the details to the interested reader.
∎

For example, setting
k=1
and
l=2p\ (p\in\mathbb{N})
in Theorem
2.12
yields

\displaystyle S(1,2p+1)=\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}(-1)^{j-1}T(2p+1-j)T(j+1)-\frac{(-1)^{p}}{2}T^{2}(p+1).

Theorem 2.13
.

For positive integers
k_{1},k_{2}
and
l
,

\displaystyle(-1)^{l-1}T((k_{1},k_{2})\circledast(1,l))+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}-1}T(1,k_{1},k_{2}+l)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T(k_{1},k_{2}+1-j)T(l+j)-\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}(-1)^{j-1}T(l+1-j)T(k_{1},k_{2}+j)

\displaystyle\quad-(-1)^{k_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T(k_{1}+1-j)S(j,k_{2}+l)-(-1)^{k_{2}}T(k_{2}+l)\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k_{1},1;x)\,dx,

(33)

where
\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k,1;x)\,dx
is given by Example
2.10
.

Proof.

From (
25
) and (
26
), we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-1}{\rm A}(k;x)\,dx=\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n)^{j}}T(k+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{k-1}}{(2n)^{k}}T_{n}(1)

and

\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}{\rm A}(k_{1},k_{2};x)\,dx=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}T(k_{1},k_{2}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2}}}T_{n}(1,k_{1})\\
+\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}-1}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2}}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T(k_{1}+1-j)S_{n}(j)+\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}-1}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k_{1},1,;x)\,dx.

According to the definitions of A-functions, MTVs and MSVs, on the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm A}(k_{1},k_{2};x){\rm A}(l;x)}{x}\,dx=2\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{l}}\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}{\rm A}(k_{1},k_{2};x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T(k_{1},k_{2}+1-j)T(l+j)-(-1)^{k_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}T(k_{1}+1-j)S(j,k_{2}+l)

\displaystyle\quad-(-1)^{k_{2}}T(k_{2}+l)\int_{0}^{1}{\rm A}(k_{1},1;x)\,dx+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}T(1,k_{1},k_{2}+l).

On the other hand,

\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm A}(k_{1},k_{2};x){\rm A}(l;x)}{x}\,dx=2\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{T_{n}(k_{1})}{(2n)^{k_{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-1}{\rm A}(l;x)\,dx\\
=\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}(-1)^{j-1}T(l+1-j)T(k_{1},k_{2}+j)+(-1)^{l-1}T((k_{1},k_{2})\circledast(1,l)).

Hence, combining two identities above, we obtain the desired evaluation.
∎

Theorem 2.14
.

For positive integers
k_{1},k_{2}
and
l_{1},l_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}T((l_{1},l_{2})\circledast(1,k_{1},k_{2}))-(-1)^{l_{1}+l_{2}}T((k_{1},k_{2})\circledast(1,l_{1},l_{2}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j}T(k_{1},k_{2}+1-j)T(l_{1},l_{2}+j)-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}-1}(-1)^{j}T(l_{1},l_{2}+1-j)T(k_{1},k_{2}+j)

\displaystyle\quad-(-1)^{k_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}(-1)^{j}T(k_{1}+1-j)T((l_{1},l_{2})\circledast(j,k_{2}))

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{l_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{1}}(-1)^{j}T(l_{1}+1-j)T((k_{1},k_{2})\circledast(j,l_{2})),

where
T(1):=2\log(2).

Proof.

Consider the integral

\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm A}(k_{1},k_{2};x){\rm A}(l_{1},l_{2};x)}{x}\,dx.

By a similar argument used in the proof of Theorem
2.13
, we can prove Theorem
2.14
.
∎

Moreover, according to the definitions of Kaneko–Tsumura
\psi
-function and Kaneko–Tsumura A-function (which is a single-variable multiple polylogarithm function of level two)
[
6
,
7
]
,

\displaystyle\psi(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};s):=\frac{1}{\Gamma(s)}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{s-1}}{\sinh(t)}{\rm A}({k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}};\tanh(t/2))dt\quad(\Re(s)>0)

(34)

and

\displaystyle{\rm A}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};z):=2^{r}\sum\limits_{1\leq{n_{1}}<\cdots<{n_{r}}\atop n_{i}\equiv i\ {\rm mod}\ 2}{\frac{{{z^{{n_{r}}}}}}{{n_{1}^{{k_{1}}}\cdots n_{r}^{{k_{r}}}}}},\quad z\in\left[{-1,1}\right).

(35)

Setting
\tanh(t/2)=x
and
s=p+1\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\psi(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};p+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{p}}{p!}\int\limits_{0}^{1}\frac{\log^{p}\left(\frac{1-x}{1+x}\right){\rm A}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x)}{x}dx

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm A}(\{1\}_{p};x){\rm A}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x)}{x}dx,

(36)

where we have used the relation

\displaystyle{\rm A}({\{1\}_{r}};x)=\frac{1}{r!}({\rm A}(1;x))^{r}=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{r!}\log^{r}\left(\frac{1-x}{1+x}\right).

We remark that the Kaneko–Tsumura
\psi
-values can be regarded as a special case of the integral
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
. So, one can prove
[
13
, Theorem 3.3]
by considering the integrals
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
.

2.3
Multiple integrals associated with 2-labeled posets

According to iterated integral expressions, we know that
{\rm Li}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}(x)}
and
{\rm A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};x)
satisfy the shuffle product relation. In this subsection, we will express integrals
I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
and
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
in terms of multiple integral associated with 2-labeled posets, which implies that the integrals
I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
and
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
can be expressed in terms of linear combination of MZVs (or MTVs). The key properties of these integrals was first studied by Yamamoto in
[
14
]
.

Definition 2.1
.

A
2
-poset
is a pair
(X,\delta_{X})
, where
X=(X,\leq)
is
a finite partially ordered set and
\delta_{X}
is a map from
X
to
\{0,1\}
.
We often omit
\delta_{X}
and simply say “a 2-poset
X
”.
The
\delta_{X}
is called the
label map
of
X
.

A 2-poset
(X,\delta_{X})
is called
admissible
if

\delta_{X}(x)=0
for all maximal elements
x\in X
and

\delta_{X}(x)=1
for all minimal elements
x\in X
.

Definition 2.2
.

For an admissible 2-poset
X
, we define the associated integral

I_{j}(X)=\int_{\Delta_{X}}\prod_{x\in X}{\omega}^{(j)}_{\delta_{X}(x)}(t_{x}),\qquad j=1,2,

(37)

where

\Delta_{X}=\bigl\{(t_{x})_{x}\in[0,1]^{X}\bigm|t_{x}<t_{y}\text{ if }x<y\bigr\}

and

{\omega}^{(1)}_{0}(t)={\omega}^{(2)}_{0}(t)=\frac{dt}{t},\quad{\omega}^{(1)}_{1}(t)=\frac{dt}{1-t},\quad{\omega}^{(2)}_{1}(t)=\frac{2dt}{1-t^{2}}.

For the empty 2-poset, denoted
\emptyset
, we put
I_{j}(\emptyset):=1\ (j=1,2)
.

Proposition 2.15
.

For non-comparable elements
a
and
b
of a
2
-poset
X
,
X^{b}_{a}
denotes the
2
-poset that is obtained from
X
by adjoining the relation
a<b
. If
X
is an admissible
2
-poset, then the
2
-poset
X^{b}_{a}
and
X^{a}_{b}
are admissible and

I_{j}(X)=I_{j}(X^{b}_{a})+I_{j}(X^{a}_{b})\quad(j=1,2).

(38)

Note that the admissibility of a 2-poset corresponds to
the convergence of the associated integral. We use Hasse diagrams to indicate 2-posets, with vertices
\circ
and
\bullet
corresponding to
\delta(x)=0
and
\delta(x)=1
, respectively. For example, the diagram

represents the 2-poset
X=\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5}\}
with order

x_{1}<x_{2}>x_{3}<x_{4}<x_{5}
and label

(\delta_{X}(x_{1}),\dotsc,\delta_{X}(x_{5}))=(1,0,1,0,0)
.
This 2-poset is admissible.
To describe the corresponding diagram, we introduce an abbreviation:
For a sequence
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
of positive integers,
we write

\scriptstyle{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}

for the vertical diagram
