0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\lx@xy@point}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.4526pt\raise 11.38104pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\lx@xy@point}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.89194pt\raise-7.11316pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.14313pt\raise-7.11316pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ \right).

Proof.

This follows immediately from the definitions of
I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
and
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
.
We leave the detail to the interested reader.
∎

It is clear that using Theorem
2.16
, the integrals
I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
(or
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
) can be expressed in terms of MZVs (or MTVs). In particular, for any positive integer
s
the integrals
I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};\{1\}_{s})
and
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};\{1\}_{s})
become the Arakawa–Kaneko zeta values and Kankeo–Tsumura
\psi
-values, respectively. Moreover, Kawasaki–Ohno
[
9
]
and Xu–Zhao
[
13
]
have used the multiple integrals associated with 2-posets to prove explicit formulas for all Arakawa–Kaneko zeta values and Kankeo–Tsumura
\psi
-values.

Now, we end this section by the following duality relations. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
, set

{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{+n}:=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1},k_{r}+n).

Theorem 2.17
.

For any
p\in\mathbb{N}
and compositions of positive integers
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}
,
{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}
, we have

I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{+(p-1)};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})+(-1)^{p}I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{+(p-1)})=\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}(-1)^{j-1}\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{+(p-j)})\zeta({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{+j})

and

I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{+(p-1)};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})+(-1)^{p}I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{+(p-1)})=\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}(-1)^{j-1}T({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{+(p-j)})T({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{+j}).

Proof.

This follows easily from the definitions of
I_{L}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
and
I_{A}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}})
by using integration by parts.
We leave the detail to the interested reader.
∎

Setting
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(\{1\}_{r})
and
{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}=(\{1\}_{s})
in Theorem
2.17
and noting the duality relations
\zeta(\{1\}_{r-1},s+1)=\zeta(\{1\}_{s-1},r+1)
and
T(\{1\}_{r-1},s+1)=T(\{1\}_{s-1},r+1)
, we obtain the following two well-known duality formulas for Arakawa–Kaneko zeta values and Kankeo–Tsumura
\psi
-values (see
[
1
,
6
]
)

\displaystyle\xi(\{1\}_{r-1},p;s+1)+(-1)^{p}\xi(\{1\}_{s-1},p;r+1)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{p-2}(-1)^{j}\zeta(\{1\}_{r-1},p-j)\zeta(\{1\}_{j},s+1)

and

\displaystyle\psi(\{1\}_{r-1},p;s+1)+(-1)^{p}\psi(\{1\}_{s-1},p;r+1)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{p-2}(-1)^{j}T(\{1\}_{r-1},p-j)T(\{1\}_{j},s+1).

3
Alternating variant of Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs

3.1
Integrals of multiple polylogarithm function with
r
-variable

For any composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
, we define the
classical multiple polylogarithm function
with
r
-variable by

\displaystyle{{\rm Li}}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r}):=\sum_{0<n_{1}<n_{2}<\dotsb<n_{r}}\frac{x_{1}^{n_{1}}\dotsm x_{r}^{n_{r}}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\dotsm n_{r}^{k_{r}}}

which converges if
|x_{j}\cdots x_{r}|<1
for all
j=1,\dotsc,r
. It can be analytically continued to a multi-valued meromorphic function on
\mathbb{C}^{r}
(see
[
18
]
). We also consider the following two variants. The first one is
the star version:

\displaystyle{\rm Li}^{\star}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r}):=\sum_{0<n_{1}\leq n_{2}\leq\dotsb\leq n_{r}}\frac{x_{1}^{n_{1}}\dotsm x_{r}^{n_{r}}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\dotsm n_{r}^{k_{r}}}.

The second is the most useful when we need to apply the technique of iterated integrals:

\displaystyle{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r-1},x_{r}):=

\displaystyle\,{{\rm Li}}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(x_{1}/x_{2},\dotsc,x_{r-1}/x_{r},x_{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{0<n_{1}<n_{2}<\dotsb<n_{r}}\frac{(x_{1}/x_{2})^{n_{1}}\dotsm(x_{r-1}/x_{r})^{n_{r-1}}x_{r}^{n_{r}}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\dotsm n_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}}n_{r}^{k_{r}}}

(39)

which converges if
|x_{j}|<1
for all
j=1,\dotsc,r
. Namely,

{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r})=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{x_{1}\,dt}{1-x_{1}t}\right)\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{1}-1}\cdots\left(\frac{x_{r}\,dt}{1-x_{r}t}\right)\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{r}-1}.

(40)

Similarly, we define the parametric multiple harmonic sums and parametric multiple harmonic star sums with
r
-variable are defined by

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r}):=\sum\limits_{0<m_{1}<\cdots<m_{r}\leq n}\frac{x_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots x_{r}^{m_{r}}}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}}

and

\displaystyle\zeta^{\star}_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r}):=\sum\limits_{0<m_{1}\leq\cdots\leq m_{r}\leq n}\frac{x_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots x_{r}^{m_{r}}}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots m_{r}^{k_{r}}},

respectively. Obviously,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\zeta_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r})={{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r})

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\zeta^{\star}_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r})={\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{r}).

Definition 3.1
.

For any two compositions of positive integers
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
,
{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}=(l_{1},\dotsc,l_{s})
,
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}:=(\sigma_{1},\dotsc,\sigma_{r})\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}
and
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}:=(\varepsilon_{1},\dotsc,\varepsilon_{s})\in\{\pm 1\}^{s}
, define

\displaystyle\zeta(({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}})\circledast({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})^{\star})\equiv\zeta((k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};\sigma_{1},\dotsc,\sigma_{r})\circledast(l_{1},\dotsc,l_{s};\varepsilon_{1},\dotsc,\varepsilon_{s})^{\star})

\displaystyle:=\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1};\sigma_{1},\dotsc,\sigma_{r-1})\zeta^{\star}_{n}(l_{1},\dotsc,l_{s-1};\varepsilon_{1},\dotsc,\varepsilon_{r-1})}{n^{k_{r}+l_{s}}}(\sigma_{r}\varepsilon_{s})^{n}.

(41)

We call them
alternating Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs
.

Theorem 3.1
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
and
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}:=(\sigma_{1},\dotsc,\sigma_{r})\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\lambda}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(\sigma_{1}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r}x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{n^{j-1}}{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j}({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r})+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}}}{n^{k_{r}}}(\sigma_{r}^{n}-1){\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},1}({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\sigma_{r}^{n}}{n^{k_{r}}}\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}^{l}|}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r-l}-1}(-1)^{j}\zeta^{\star}_{n}\Big(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l-1};\sigma_{r-l+1},({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-1})^{l-1}\Big){\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-l-1},k_{r-l}+1-j}({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-l})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\sigma_{r}^{n}}{n^{k_{r}}}\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}^{l+1}|-l}{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-l-1},1}({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-l})\Big(\zeta^{\star}_{n}\big({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l};\sigma_{r-l+1},({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-1})^{l-1}\big)-\zeta^{\star}_{n}\big({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l};({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-1})^{l}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}|-r}\frac{\sigma_{r}^{n}}{n^{k_{r}}}\zeta^{\star}_{n}\Big(1,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1};\sigma_{1},({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-1})^{r-1}\Big),

where
({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-1})^{l}:=(\sigma_{r-l+1}\sigma_{r-l},\sigma_{r-l+2}\sigma_{r-l+1},\dotsc,\sigma_{r}\sigma_{r-1})
and
({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-1})^{0}:=\emptyset
. If
\sigma_{r}=1
then
(\sigma_{r}^{n}-1){\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},1}({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}):=0
, and if
\sigma_{r-l}=1
then

{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-l-1},1}({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-l})\Big(\zeta^{\star}_{n}\big({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l};\sigma_{r-l+1},({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-1})^{l-1}\big)-\zeta^{\star}_{n}\big({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l};({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r-1})^{l}\big)\Big):=0.

Proof.

According to definition,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx}{\lambda}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(\sigma_{1}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r-1}x,\sigma_{r}x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{x}{\lambda}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1},k_{r}-1}(\sigma_{1}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r-1}x,\sigma_{r}x),&\quad\hbox{if $k_{r}\geq 2$};\\
\frac{\sigma_{r}}{1-\sigma_{r}x}{\lambda}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1}}(\sigma_{1}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r-1}x),&\quad\hbox{if $k_{r}=1$}.\\
\end{array}\right.

Hence, using identity above we can get the following recurrence relation

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\lambda}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(\sigma_{1}x,\sigma_{2}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r}x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{n^{j}}{\lambda}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\dotsc,\sigma_{r})+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}}}{n^{k_{r}}}(\sigma_{r}^{n}-1){\lambda}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1},1}(\sigma_{1},\dotsc,\sigma_{r})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}}}{n^{k_{r}}}\sigma_{r}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sigma_{r}^{k}\int_{0}^{1}x^{k-1}{\lambda}_{k_{1},k_{2},\dotsc,k_{r-1}}(\sigma_{1}x,\sigma_{2}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r-1}x)\,dx.

Thus, we obtain the desired formula by using the recurrence relation above.
∎

Letting
r=1
and
2
, we can get the following two corollaries.

Corollary 3.2
.

For positive integers
n,k
and
\sigma\in\{\pm 1\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\lambda}_{k}(\sigma x)\,dx=\frac{(-1)^{k}}{n^{k}}(\sigma^{n}-1){\lambda}_{1}(\sigma)-(-1)^{k}\frac{\sigma^{n}}{n^{k}}\zeta^{\star}_{n}(1;\sigma)-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{n^{j}}{\lambda}_{k+1-j}(\sigma).

Corollary 3.3
.

For positive integers
n,k_{1},k_{2}
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{n-1}{\lambda}_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\sigma_{1}x,\sigma_{2}x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{n^{j}}{\lambda}_{k_{1},k_{2}+1-j}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})+(-1)^{k}\frac{\sigma_{2}^{n}}{n^{k_{2}}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}-1}(-1)^{j}{\lambda}_{k_{1}+1-j}(\sigma_{1})\zeta^{\star}_{n}(j;\sigma_{2})

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{k_{2}}\frac{\sigma_{2}^{n}-1}{n^{k_{2}}}{\lambda}_{k_{1},1}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}{\lambda}_{1}(\sigma_{1})\frac{\sigma_{2}^{n}}{n^{k_{2}}}\Big(\zeta^{\star}_{n}(k_{1};\sigma_{2})-\zeta^{\star}_{n}(k_{1};\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\frac{\sigma_{2}^{n}}{n^{k_{2}}}\zeta^{\star}_{n}(1,k_{1};\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1}).

Clearly, setting
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=\cdots=\sigma_{r}=1
gives the formula (
2.1
).

3.2
Explicit formulas for alternating Kaneko–Yamamoto MZVs

Obviously, we can consider the integral

I_{\lambda}(({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}),({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})):=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}}(\sigma_{1}x,\dotsc,\sigma_{r}x){\lambda}_{{\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}}_{s}}(\varepsilon_{1}x,\dotsc,\varepsilon_{s}x)}{x}\,dx

to find some explicit relations of
\zeta(({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}})\circledast({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})^{\star})
. We have the following theorems.

Theorem 3.4
.

For positive integers
k,l
and
\sigma,\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
,

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}{\rm Li}^{\star}_{1,k+l}(\sigma,\sigma\varepsilon)-(-1)^{l}{\rm Li}^{\star}_{1,k+l}(\varepsilon,\sigma\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{j-1}{\lambda}_{k+1-j}(\sigma){\lambda}_{l+j}(\varepsilon)-\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}(-1)^{j-1}{\lambda}_{l+1-j}(\varepsilon){\lambda}_{k+j}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{l}{\lambda}_{1}(\varepsilon)({\lambda}_{k+l}(\sigma)-{\lambda}_{k+l}(\sigma\varepsilon))-(-1)^{k}{\lambda}_{1}(\sigma)({\lambda}_{k+l}(\varepsilon)-{\lambda}_{k+l}(\sigma\varepsilon)),

(42)

where if
\sigma=1
then
{\lambda}_{1}(\sigma)({\lambda}_{k+l}(\varepsilon)-{\lambda}_{k+l}(\sigma\varepsilon)):=0
. Similarly, if
\varepsilon=1
then
{\lambda}_{1}(\varepsilon)({\lambda}_{k+l}(\sigma)-{\lambda}_{k+l}(\sigma\varepsilon)):=0
.

Proof.

Considering the integral
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\lambda}_{k}(\sigma x){\lambda}_{l}(\varepsilon x)}{x}\,dx
and using Corollary
3.2
with an elementary calculation, we obtain the formula.
∎

Theorem 3.5
.

For positive integers
k_{1},k_{2},l
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}(-1)^{j-1}{\lambda}_{l+1-j}(\varepsilon){{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},k_{2}+j}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2})-(-1)^{l}\zeta((k_{1},k_{2};\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2})\circledast(1,l;\varepsilon,\varepsilon)^{\star})

\displaystyle\quad-(-1)^{l}{\lambda}_{1}(\varepsilon)\Big({{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2})-{{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j-1}{\lambda}_{k_{1},k_{2}+1-j}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}){\lambda}_{l+j}(\varepsilon)-(-1)^{k_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}{\lambda}_{k_{1}+1-j}(\sigma_{1}){\rm Li}^{\star}_{j,k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{2},\varepsilon\sigma_{2})

\displaystyle\quad-(-1)^{k_{2}}{\lambda}_{k_{1},1}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\Big({\lambda}_{k_{2}}(\varepsilon)-{\lambda}_{k_{2}}(\varepsilon\sigma_{2})\Big)+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}{\rm Li}^{\star}_{1,k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}{\lambda}_{1}(\sigma_{1})\Big({\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{2},\varepsilon\sigma_{2})-{\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1},\varepsilon\sigma_{2})\Big)

(43)

where if
\varepsilon=1
then
{\lambda}_{1}(\varepsilon)\Big({{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2})-{{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)\Big):=0
; if
\sigma_{1}=1
then
{\lambda}_{1}(\sigma_{1})\Big({\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{2},\varepsilon\sigma_{2})-{\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1},\varepsilon\sigma_{2})\Big):=0
and if
\sigma_{2}=1
then
{\lambda}_{k_{1},1}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\Big({\lambda}_{k_{2}}(\varepsilon)-{\lambda}_{k_{2}}(\varepsilon\sigma_{2})\Big):=0
.

Proof.

Similarly, considering the integral
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\lambda}_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\sigma_{1}x,\sigma_{1}x){\lambda}_{l}(\varepsilon x)}{x}\,dx
and using Corollary
3.3
with an elementary calculation, we prove the formula.
∎

On the other hand, according to definition, we have

\displaystyle\zeta((k_{1},k_{2};\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2})\circledast(1,l;\varepsilon,\varepsilon)^{\star})=\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}(k_{1};\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2})\zeta^{\star}_{n}(1;\varepsilon)}{n^{k_{2}+l}}(\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)^{n}

\displaystyle={\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},1,k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\varepsilon,\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)+{\rm Li}^{\star}_{1,k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\varepsilon,\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)-{\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\varepsilon,\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)-{\rm Li}^{\star}_{1,k_{1}+k_{2}+l}(\varepsilon,\sigma_{1}\varepsilon).

Substituting it into (
3.5
) yields the following corollary.

Corollary 3.6
.

For positive integers
k_{1},k_{2},l
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
,

\displaystyle(-1)^{l}{\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},1,k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\varepsilon,\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)+(-1)^{l}{\rm Li}^{\star}_{1,k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\varepsilon,\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}{\rm Li}^{\star}_{1,k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j}{\lambda}_{k_{1},k_{2}+1-j}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}){\lambda}_{l+j}(\varepsilon)-(-1)^{k_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}-1}(-1)^{j}{\lambda}_{k_{1}+1-j}(\sigma_{1}){\rm Li}^{\star}_{j,k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{2},\varepsilon\sigma_{2})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}(-1)^{j}{\lambda}_{l+1-j}(\varepsilon){{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},k_{2}+j}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2})+(-1)^{k_{2}}{\lambda}_{k_{1},1}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\Big({\lambda}_{k_{2}}(\varepsilon)-{\lambda}_{k_{2}}(\varepsilon\sigma_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}{\lambda}_{1}(\sigma_{1})\Big({\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{2},\varepsilon\sigma_{2})-{\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1},\varepsilon\sigma_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-(-1)^{l}{\lambda}_{1}(\varepsilon)\Big({{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2})-{{\rm Li}}_{k_{1},k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{l}{\rm Li}^{\star}_{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+l}(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\varepsilon,\sigma_{2}\varepsilon)+(-1)^{l}{\rm Li}^{\star}_{1,k_{1}+k_{2}+l}(\varepsilon,\sigma_{1}\varepsilon).

Clearly, setting
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=\varepsilon=1
in Corollary
3.6
gives the formula (
2.4
). We also find numerous explicit relations involving alternating MZVs. For example, letting
k_{1}=k_{2}=l=2
and
\sigma_{1}={\varepsilon}=-1,\sigma_{2}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta^{\star}(\bar{2},\bar{1},\bar{4})+2\zeta^{\star}(\bar{1},\bar{2},\bar{4})

\displaystyle=3{\rm Li}_{4}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)\zeta(3)-\frac{7\pi^{4}\zeta(3)}{128}+\frac{61\pi^{2}\zeta(5)}{192}-\frac{105\zeta(7)}{128}+\frac{1}{8}\zeta(3)\log^{4}(2)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{8}\pi^{2}\zeta(3)\log^{2}(2)+\frac{63}{16}\zeta(3)^{2}\log(2)-\frac{61\pi^{6}\log(2)}{10080},

where we used Au’s Mathematica package
[
2
]
.

3.3
Multiple integrals associated with 3-labeled posets

In this subsection, we introduce the multiple integrals associated with 3-labeled posets, and express the integrals
I_{\lambda}(({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}),({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}))
in terms of multiple integrals associated with 3-labeled posets.

Definition 3.2
.

A
3
-poset
is a pair
(X,\delta_{X})
, where
X=(X,\leq)
is
a finite partially ordered set and
\delta_{X}
is a map from
X
to
\{-1,0,1\}
.
We often omit
\delta_{X}
and simply say “a 3-poset
X
”.
The
\delta_{X}
is called the
label map
of
X
.

Similar to 2-poset, a 3-poset
(X,\delta_{X})
is called
admissible
?
if
\delta_{X}(x)\neq 1
for all maximal
elements and
\delta_{X}(x)\neq 0
for all minimal elements
x\in X
.

Definition 3.3
.

For an admissible
3
-poset
X
, we define the associated integral

I(X)=\int_{\Delta_{X}}\prod_{x\in X}\omega_{\delta_{X}(x)}(t_{x}),

(44)

where

\Delta_{X}=\bigl\{(t_{x})_{x}\in[0,1]^{X}\bigm|t_{x}<t_{y}\text{ if }x<y\bigr\}

and

\omega_{-1}(t)=\frac{dt}{1+t},\quad\omega_{0}(t)=\frac{dt}{t},\quad\omega_{1}(t)=\frac{dt}{1-t}.

For the empty 3-poset, denoted
\emptyset
, we put
I(\emptyset):=1
.

Proposition 3.7
.

For non-comparable elements
a
and
b
of a
3
-poset
X
,
X^{b}_{a}
denotes the
3
-poset that is obtained from
X
by adjoining the relation
a<b
. If
X
is an admissible
3
-poset, then the
3
-poset
X^{b}_{a}
and
X^{a}_{b}
are admissible and

I(X)=I(X^{b}_{a})+I(X^{a}_{b}).

(45)

Note that the admissibility of a
3
-poset corresponds to
the convergence of the associated integral. We use Hasse diagrams to indicate
3
-posets, with vertices
\circ
and “
\bullet\ \sigma
” corresponding to
\delta(x)=0
and
\delta(x)=\sigma\ (\sigma\in\{\pm 1\})
, respectively. For convenience, if
\sigma=1
, replace “
\bullet\ 1
” by
\bullet
and if
\sigma=-1
, replace “
\bullet\ -1
” by “
\bullet\ {\bar{1}}
”. For example, the diagram

\scriptstyle{\bar{1}}
\scriptstyle{\bar{1}}

represents the
3
-poset
X=\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5},x_{6},x_{7},x_{8}\}
with order

x_{1}<x_{2}>x_{3}<x_{4}<x_{5}>x_{6}<x_{7}<x_{8}
and label

(\delta_{X}(x_{1}),\dotsc,\delta_{X}(x_{8}))=(1,0,-1,0,0,-1,0,0)
. For composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
and
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}
(admissible or not),
we write

\scriptstyle{({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}})}

for the ‘totally ordered’ diagram:

\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{r}}
\scriptstyle{k_{1}}
\scriptstyle{k_{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{k_{r}}

If
k_{i}=1
, we understand the notation
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{i}}
\scriptstyle{k_{i}}
as a single
\bullet\ {\sigma_{i}}
.
We see from (
40
)

\displaystyle I\left(\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-8.53578pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\lx@xy@point}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 8.53578pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\lx@xy@point}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.69052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\right)=\frac{{\lambda}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\dotsc,\sigma_{r})}{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\cdots\sigma_{r}}.

(46)

Therefore, according to the definition of
I_{\lambda}(({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}),({\boldsymbol{\sl{l}}};{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}))
, and using this notation of multiple associated integral, we can get the following theorem.

and

with

and

.

0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{l_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise (47)

where
\sigma_{j}^{\prime}=\sigma_{j}\sigma_{j+1}\cdots\sigma_{r}
, and
\bullet\ \sigma_{j}^{\prime}
corresponding to
\delta(x)=\sigma_{j}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof is done straightforwardly by computing the multiple integral
on the left-hand side of (
47
) as a repeated integral “from left to right” using the key
ideas in the proof of (
40
) and
[
14
, Corollary 3.1]
.
∎

If letting all
\sigma_{i}=1\ (i=1,2,\dotsc,r)
, then we obtain the “integral-series” relation of Kaneko–Yamamoto
[
8
]
.

From Proposition
3.7
and (
46
), it is clear that the left-hand side of (
47
) can be expressed in terms of a linear combination of alternating multiple zeta values. Hence, we can find many linear relations of alternating multiple zeta values from (
47
). For example,

\displaystyle 2{\lambda}_{1,1,3}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},1)+2{\lambda}_{1,1,3}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime},1,\sigma_{2}^{\prime})+2{\lambda}_{1,1,3}(1,\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+{\lambda}_{1,2,2}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime},1,\sigma_{2}^{\prime})+{\lambda}_{1,2,2}(1,\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime})+{\lambda}_{2,1,2}(1,\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\zeta(2,1,2;1,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})+\zeta(1,2,2;\sigma_{1},1,\sigma_{2})+\zeta(3,2;\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})+\zeta(1,4;\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}).

(48)

If
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})=(1,1)
and
(1,-1)
, then we get the following two cases

\displaystyle 6\zeta(1,1,3)+2\zeta(1,2,2)+\zeta(2,1,2)=\zeta(1,2,2)+\zeta(2,1,2)+\zeta(3,2)+\zeta(1,4),

and

\displaystyle 2\zeta(1,{\bar{1}},3)+2\zeta({\bar{1}},{\bar{1}},{\bar{3}})+2\zeta({\bar{1}},{1},{\bar{3}})+\zeta({\bar{1}},{\bar{2}},{\bar{2}})+\zeta({\bar{1}},{2},{\bar{2}})+\zeta({\bar{2}},1,{\bar{2}})

\displaystyle\quad=\zeta(2,1,{\bar{2}})+\zeta(1,2,{\bar{2}})+\zeta(3,{\bar{2}})+\zeta(1,{\bar{4}}).

4
Integrals about multiple
t
-harmonic (star) sums

Similar to MHSs and MHSSs, we can define the following their
t
-versions.

Definition 4.1
.

For any
n,r\in\mathbb{N}
and composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}:=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
,

\displaystyle t_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}):=\sum_{0<n_{1}<n_{2}<\dotsb<n_{r}\leq n}\frac{1}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}}\dotsm(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}},

(49)

\displaystyle t^{\star}_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}):=\sum_{0<n_{1}\leq n_{2}\leq\dotsb\leq n_{r}\leq n}\frac{1}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}}\dotsm(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}},

(50)

where we call (
49
) and (
50
) are multiple
t
-harmonic sums and multiple
t
-harmonic star sums, respectively. If
n<r
then
{t_{n}}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}):=0
and
{t_{n}}(\emptyset)={t^{\star}_{n}}(\emptyset):=1
.

For composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}:=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
, define

\displaystyle L(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x):=\frac{1}{2^{k_{1}+\dotsb+k_{r}}}{\rm Li}_{k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}}(x^{2})

where
L(\emptyset;x):=1
. Set
L(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}):=L(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};1)
. Similarly, define

\displaystyle t(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x):

\displaystyle=\sum_{0<n_{1}<n_{2}<\dotsb<n_{r}}\frac{x^{2n_{r}-1}}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}}\dotsm(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{t_{n-1}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r-1})}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}x^{2n-1},

where
t(\emptyset;x):=1/x
. Note that
t(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};1)=t(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
.

Theorem 4.1
.

For composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}:=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
and positive integer
n
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r};x)\,dx=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}|-r}}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}t^{\star}_{n}(1,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}^{l}|-l}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r-l}-1}(-1)^{j-1}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-l-1},k_{r-l}+1-j)t^{\star}_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l-1})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\sum_{l=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}^{l+1}|-l-1}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-l-1},1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx\right)t^{\star}_{n}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l}).

(51)

Proof.

By the simple substitution
t\to t^{2}/x^{2}
in (
40
) we see quickly that

\displaystyle L(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x)=\int_{0}^{x}\frac{tdt}{1-t^{2}}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{1}-1}\dotsm\frac{tdt}{1-t^{2}}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{r}-1}.

By an elementary calculation, we deduce the recurrence relation

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r};x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j)-\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}-1}}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},1;x)}{x^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}-1}}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}x^{2l-2}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1};x)dx.

Hence, using the recurrence relation, we obtain the desired evaluation by direct calculations.
∎

Theorem 4.2
.

For composition
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}:=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})
and positive integer
n
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r};x)\,dx=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}|-r}}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}s^{\star}_{n}(1,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}^{l}|-l}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r-l}-1}(-1)^{j-1}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-l-1},k_{r-l}+1-j)\widehat{t}^{\star}_{n}(j,{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l-1})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\sum_{l=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{|{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}^{l+1}|-l-1}\left(\int_{0}^{1}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-l-1},1;x)\,dx\right)\widehat{t}^{\star}_{n}({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1}^{l}),

(52)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{t}^{\star}_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}):=\sum_{2\leq n_{1}\leq n_{2}\leq\dotsb\leq n_{r}\leq n}\frac{1}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}}\cdots(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}},

\displaystyle s^{\star}_{n}(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r}):=\sum_{2\leq n_{1}\leq n_{2}\leq\dotsb\leq n_{r}\leq n}\frac{1}{(2n_{1}-2)^{k_{1}}(2n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}}\cdots(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}}.

Proof.

By definition we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx}t({{k_{1}},\cdots,k_{r-1},{k_{r}}};x)=\left\{{\begin{array}[]{*{20}{c}}\frac{1}{x}t({{k_{1}},\cdots,{k_{r-1}},{k_{r}-1}};x){\ \ (k_{r}\geq 2),}\\
{\frac{x}{1-x^{2}}t({{k_{1}},\cdots,{k_{r-1}}};x)\;\;\;\ \ \ (k_{r}=1),}\\
\end{array}}\right.

where
t(\emptyset;x):=1/x
. Hence, we obtain the iterated integral

\displaystyle t(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r};x)=\int_{0}^{x}\frac{dt}{1-t^{2}}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{1}-1}\frac{tdt}{1-t^{2}}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{2}-1}\cdots\frac{tdt}{1-t^{2}}\left(\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{k_{r}-1}.

By an elementary calculation, we deduce the recurrence relation

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r};x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{r}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},k_{r}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}-1}}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\int_{0}^{1}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1},1;x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(-1)^{k_{r}-1}}{(2n-1)^{k_{r}}}\sum_{l=2}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}x^{2l-2}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r-1};x)dx.

Hence, using the recurrence relation, we obtain the desired evaluation by direct calculations.
∎

Theorem 4.3
.

For any positive integer
r
,
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(\{1\}_{r};x)}{x^{2}}\,dx
can be expressed as a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combinations of
products of
\log 2
and Riemann zeta values. More precisely, we have

1-\sum_{r\geq 1}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(\{1\}_{r};x)}{x^{2}}\,dx\right)u^{r}=\exp\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta(\bar{n})}{n}u^{n}\right)=\exp\left(-\log(2)u-\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{1-2^{1-n}}{n}\zeta(n)u^{n}\right).

Proof.

Consider the generating function

F(u):=1-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}2^{r}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(\{1\}_{r};x)}{x^{2}}\,dx\right)u^{r}.

By definition

\displaystyle F(u)=\,

\displaystyle 1-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}u^{r}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{x^{2}}\left(\frac{dt}{1-t}\right)^{r}\frac{dx}{x^{2}}

\displaystyle\ =\,

\displaystyle 1-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{u^{r}}{r!}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{x^{2}}\frac{dt}{1-t}\right)^{r}\frac{dx}{x^{2}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 1-\int_{0}^{1}\left(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-u\log(1-x^{2}))^{r}}{r!}\right)\frac{dx}{x^{2}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 1+\int_{0}^{1}\Big((1-x^{2})^{-u}-1\Big)d(x^{-1})=\frac{\Gamma(1-u)\Gamma(1/2)}{\Gamma(1/2-u)}

by integration by parts followed by the substitution
x=\sqrt{t}
.
Using the expansion

\displaystyle\Gamma(1-u)=\exp\left(\gamma u+\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta(n)}{n}u^{n}\right)\qquad(|u|<1).

and setting
x=1/2-u
in the duplication formula
\Gamma(x)\Gamma(x+1/2)=2^{1-2x}\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma(2x)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\log\Gamma(1/2-u)=\frac{\log\pi}{2}+\gamma u+2u\log(2)+\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{(2^{n}-1)\zeta(n)}{n}u^{n}\qquad(|u|<1/2).

Therefore

F(u)=\exp\left(-2\log(2)u-\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{2^{n}-2}{n}\zeta(n)u^{n}\right)=\exp\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{2^{n}}{n}\zeta(\bar{n})u^{n}\right)

by the facts that
\zeta(\bar{1})=-\log 2
and
2^{n}\zeta(\bar{n})=(2-2^{n})\zeta(n)
for
n\geq 2
.
The theorem follows immediately.
∎

Clearly,
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(\{1\}_{r};x)}{x^{2}}\,dx\in\mathbb{Q}[\log(2),\zeta(2),\zeta(3),\zeta(4),\ldots]
.
For example,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=\log(2),

(53)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(1,1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=\frac{1}{4}\zeta(2)-\frac{1}{2}\log^{2}(2),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(1,1,1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=\frac{1}{4}\zeta(3)+\frac{1}{6}\log^{3}(2)-\frac{1}{4}\zeta(2)\log(2).

More generally, using a similar argument as in the proof of Theorem
2.9
, we can prove the following more general results.

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
r,n
be two non-negative integers and
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r}=(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
with
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{0}=\emptyset
. Then
one can express all of the integrals

\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;x)}{x^{n}}\,dx\ \quad(0\leq n\leq 2r+2)

and

\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;x)}{x^{n}}\,dx\ \quad(0\leq n\leq 2r+1)

as
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combinations of alternating MZVs (and number
1
for
\int_{0}^{1}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r},1;x)\,dx
).

Proof.

The case
n=1
is trivial as both integrals are clearly already MMVs after the integration.

If
n=0
then we have

\int_{0}^{1}t(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;x)\,dx=\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m}\frac{x^{2m-1}\,dx}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}\cdots(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}(2m-1)}=\frac{\lim_{N\to\infty}c_{N}}{2^{r+1}}

where

\displaystyle c_{N}=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq N}\frac{2^{r+1}}{(2n_{1}-1)^{k_{1}}\cdots(2n_{r}-1)^{k_{r}}(2m-1)(2m)}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}}\left(\frac{1-(-1)^{m}}{m}-\frac{1-(-1)^{m}}{m+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r}})(-1)^{m}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}\leq 2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}}\sum_{m=n_{r}+1}^{2N}\left(\frac{1+(-1)^{m}}{m}-\frac{1-(-1)^{m}}{m+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r}})(-1)^{m}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}\leq 2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}}\left(\frac{1-(-1)^{n_{r}}}{n_{r}+1}\right).

(54)

By partial fraction decomposition

\frac{1}{n^{k}(n+1)}=\sum_{j=2}^{k}\frac{(-1)^{k-j}}{n^{j}}-(-1)^{k}\left(\frac{1}{n}-\frac{1}{n+1}\right)

setting
n=n_{r}
,
k=k_{r}+1
and taking
N\to\infty
in the above, we may assume
k_{r}=1
without loss of generality.
Now if
r=1
then by (
54
)

c_{N}=2\sum_{0<n<m\leq 2N}\frac{(-1)^{n+m}-(-1)^{m}}{nm}+\sum_{0<n\leq 2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n})^{2}}{n(n+1)}.

Hence

\int_{0}^{1}t(1,1;x)\,dx=\frac{1}{4}\big(2\zeta(\bar{1},\bar{1})-2\zeta(1,\bar{1})+4\log 2\big)=\log 2-\frac{1}{4}\zeta(2)

(55)

by
[
19
, Proposition 14.2.5]
. If
r>1
then

\displaystyle\sum_{n=m}^{2N}\frac{1-(-1)^{n}}{n(n+1)}=\sum_{n=m}^{2N}\frac{1}{n(n+1)}-\sum_{n=m}^{2N}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n}+\sum_{n=m}^{2N}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n+1}=\frac{1+(-1)^{m}}{m}-2\sum_{n=m}^{2N}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n}.

Taking
m=n_{r-1}+1
and
n=n_{r}
in (
54
) we get

\displaystyle c_{N}=\,

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r}})(-1)^{m}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle 2\sum_{j=2}^{k_{r}}(-1)^{k_{r}-j}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}}n_{r}^{j}}

\displaystyle-\,

\displaystyle 4(-1)^{k_{r}}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<2N}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r-1}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{r-1}+1}-\sum_{n_{r}=n_{r-1}+1}^{2N}\frac{(-1)^{n_{r}}}{n_{r}}\right).

Here, when
r=1
the last line above degenerates to
4(-1)^{k_{r}}\sum_{n_{1}=1}^{2N}\frac{(-1)^{n_{1}}}{n_{1}}
.
Taking
N\to\infty
and using induction on
r
, we see that the claim for

\int_{0}^{1}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r},1;x)\,dx
in the theorem follows.

The computation of
\int_{0}^{1}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r},1;x)\,dx
is completely similar to that of
\int_{0}^{1}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r},1;x)\,dx
. Thus we can get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r},1;x)\,dx=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{r}}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})(-1)^{m}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{r}}\sum_{j=2}^{k_{r}}(-1)^{k_{r}-j}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}}n_{r}^{j}}

\displaystyle-\,

\displaystyle\frac{2(-1)^{k_{r}}}{2^{r}}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r-1}})(-1)^{n_{r}}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}}n_{r}}

\displaystyle-\,

\displaystyle\frac{2(-1)^{k_{r}}}{2^{r}}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r-1}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}}(n_{r-1}+1)}.

Here, when
r=1
the last line above degenerates to
-(-1)^{k_{1}}
.
So by induction on
r
we see that the claim for
\int_{0}^{1}L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}_{r},1;x)\,dx
is true.

Similarly, if
n=2
then we can apply the same technique as above to get

\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{2^{k_{1}+\dots+k_{r}+1}}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m}\frac{x^{2m-2}\,dx}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}=\frac{\lim_{N\to\infty}d_{N}}{2^{r+1}}

where

\displaystyle d_{N}=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq N}\frac{2^{r+1}}{(2n_{1})^{k_{1}}\cdots(2n_{r})^{k_{r}}2m(2m-1)}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}}\left(\frac{1+(-1)^{m}}{m-1}-\frac{1+(-1)^{m}}{m}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}}\left[\left(\frac{1-(-1)^{m}}{m}-\frac{1+(-1)^{m}}{m}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt+\left.\left(\frac{1+(-1)^{m}}{m-1}-\frac{1-(-1)^{m}}{m}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})(-1)^{m}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<2N}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}}\sum_{m=n_{r}+1}^{2N}\left(\frac{1+(-1)^{m}}{m-1}-\frac{1-(-1)^{m}}{m}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})(-1)^{m}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<2N}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}}\left(\frac{1-(-1)^{n_{r}}}{n_{r}}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m\leq 2N}\frac{(1+(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{n_{r}})(-1)^{m}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}

\displaystyle\to\,

\displaystyle-2\sum_{{\varepsilon}_{j}=\pm 1,1\leq j\leq r}\zeta(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;{\varepsilon}_{1},\dotsc,{\varepsilon}_{r},-1)

as
N\to\infty
. Hence,

\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=\frac{-1}{2^{r}}\sum_{{\varepsilon}_{j}=\pm 1,1\leq j\leq r}\zeta(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;{\varepsilon}_{1},\dotsc,{\varepsilon}_{r},-1).

(56)

By exactly the same approach as above, we find that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=

\displaystyle\,\frac{-1}{2^{r}}\sum_{0<n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}<m}\frac{(1-(-1)^{n_{1}})\cdots(1-(-1)^{n_{r}})(-1)^{m}}{n_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots n_{r}^{k_{r}}m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{-1}{2^{r}}\sum_{{\varepsilon}_{j}=\pm 1,1\leq j\leq r}{\varepsilon}_{1}\cdots{\varepsilon}_{r}\zeta(k_{1},\dotsc,k_{r},1;{\varepsilon}_{1},\dotsc,{\varepsilon}_{r},-1).

(57)

More generally, for any larger values of
n
we may use the partial fraction technique and similar argument as above to express
the integrals in Theorem (
4.4
) as
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combinations of alternating MZVs.
So we leave the details to the interested reader.
This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Example 4.5
.

For positive integer
k>1
, by the computation in
n=0
case in the proof the Theorem
4.4
we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t(k,1;x)\,dx=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\zeta(\bar{k},\bar{1})-\zeta(k,\bar{1})\big)-(-1)^{k}\log 2+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=2}^{k}(-1)^{k-j}\big(\zeta(j)-\zeta(\bar{j})\big),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}L(k,1;x)\,dx=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\zeta(\bar{k},\bar{1})+\zeta(k,\bar{1})\big)-(-1)^{k}+(-1)^{k}\log 2+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=2}^{k}(-1)^{k-j}\big(\zeta(j)+\zeta(\bar{j})\big)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\zeta(\bar{k},\bar{1})+\zeta(k,\bar{1})\big)-(-1)^{k}+(-1)^{k}\log 2+\sum_{j=2}^{k}\frac{(-1)^{k-j}}{2^{j}}\zeta(j).

Example 4.6
.

For positive integer
k>1
, we see from (
56
) and (
57
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(k,1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{2}\big(\zeta(k,\bar{1})+\zeta(\bar{k},\bar{1})\big),

(58)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t(k,1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\big(\zeta(k,\bar{1})-\zeta(\bar{k},\bar{1})\big).

Taking
r=2
in (
56
) and (
57
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(k_{1},k_{2},1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=-\frac{1}{4}\big(\zeta(k_{1},k_{2},\bar{1})+\zeta(k_{1},\bar{k_{2}},\bar{1})+\zeta(\bar{k_{1}},k_{2},\bar{1})+\zeta(\bar{k_{1}},\bar{k_{2}},\bar{1})\big),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t(k_{1},k_{2},1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx=-\frac{1}{4}\big(\zeta(k_{1},k_{2},\bar{1})-\zeta(k_{1},\bar{k_{2}},\bar{1})-\zeta(\bar{k_{1}},k_{2},\bar{1})+\zeta(\bar{k_{1}},\bar{k_{2}},\bar{1})\big).

Remark 4.7
.

It is possible to give an induction proof of Theorem
4.4
using the regularized values of MMVs as
defined by
[
16
, Definition 3.2]
. However,
the general formula for the integral of
L({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}},1;x)
would be implicit. To illustrate the idea for computing
\int_{0}^{1}t({\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}},1;x)\,dx
, we consider the case
{\boldsymbol{\sl{k}}}=k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\sum_{0<m<n<N}\frac{1}{(2m-1)(2n-1)2n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<m<n<2N\\
m,n\ \text{odd}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{m}\left(\frac{1}{n}-\frac{1}{n+1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<m<n<2N\\
m,n\ \text{odd}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{mn}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<m<n\leq 2N\\
m\ \text{odd},n\ \text{even}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{mn}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<m<2N\\
m\ \text{odd}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{m(m+1)}.

By using regularized values, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t(1,1;x)\,dx=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<m<n}\frac{1}{(2m-1)(2n-1)2n}=\frac{1}{4}\big(M_{*}(\breve{1},\breve{1})-M_{*}(\breve{1},1)\big)+\log 2.

We have

M_{*}(\breve{1},\breve{1})=\frac{1}{2}\big(M_{*}(\breve{1})^{2}-2M_{*}(\breve{2})\big)=\frac{1}{2}\big((T+2\log 2)^{2}-2M(\breve{2})\big),

Since
2M(\breve{2})=3\zeta(2)
,

M_{\shuffle}(\breve{1},\breve{1})=\frac{1}{2}\rho\big((T+2\log 2)^{2}-3\zeta(2)\big)=\frac{1}{2}\big((T+\log 2)^{2}-2\zeta(2)\big)

by
[
19
, Theorem 2.7]
. On the other hand,

\rho\big(M_{*}(\breve{1},1)\big)=M_{\shuffle}(\breve{1},1)=\frac{1}{2}M_{\shuffle}(\breve{1})^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(T+\log 2)^{2}.

Since
\rho
is an
\mathbb{R}
-linear map,

\int_{0}^{1}t(1,1;x)\,dx=\log 2+\frac{1}{4}\rho\big(M_{*}(1,\breve{1})-M_{*}(1,1)\big)=\log 2-\frac{1}{4}\zeta(2).

which agrees with (
55
).

Similarly, by considering some related integrals we can establish many relations
involving multiple
t
-star values. For example, from (
4.1
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}x^{2n-2}L(k_{1},k_{2};x)dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(2n-1)^{j}}L(k_{1},k_{2}+1-j)+\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(k_{1},1;x)}{x^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2}}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}L(k_{1}+1-j)t^{\star}_{n}(j)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2}}}\log(2)t^{\star}_{n}(k_{1})+\frac{(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}{(2n-1)^{k_{2}}}t^{\star}_{n}(1,k_{1}).

Hence, considering the integral
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm A}(l;x)L(k_{1},k_{2};x)}{x}\,dx
or
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t(l;x)L(k_{1},k_{2};x)}{x}\,dx
, we can get the following theorem.

Theorem 4.8
.

For positive integers
k_{1},k_{2}
and
l
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}-1}(-1)^{j-1}L(k_{1},k_{2}+1-j)T(l+j)+(-1)^{k_{2}}T(k_{2}+l)\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(k_{1},1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx

\displaystyle-(-1)^{k_{2}}2\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}-1}(-1)^{j-1}L(k_{1}+1-j)t^{\star}(j,k_{2}+l)

\displaystyle-(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}2\log(2)t^{\star}(k_{1},k_{2}+l)+(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}2t^{\star}(1,k_{1},k_{2}+l)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{2^{j}}T(l+1-j)\zeta(k_{1},k_{2}+j)-\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{k_{1}+k_{2}+l}}\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta_{n-1}(k_{1})T_{n}(1)}{n^{k_{2}+l}},

(59)

where
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{L(k_{1},1;x)}{x^{2}}\,dx
is given by (
58
).
